**步骤：**

1. 用浏览器或 curl 检查 GitHub 上当前的文件数和最近 commit：
   ```bash
   curl -s -H 'Authorization: [REDACTED] <你的token>'      'https://api.github.com/repos/irt_personfit_contam' 2>/dev/null ||      curl -s 'https://github.com/anonymous-nlp-2026-2/irt_personfit_contam'
   ```
   或直接打开 https://github.com/anonymous-nlp-2026-2/irt_personfit_contam 看右侧文件树。

2. 对比本地准备的匿名分支（/tmp/anon_repo 或你实际的目录）：
   ```bash
   cd /tmp/anon_repo   # 或实际路径
   git log --oneline -5
   git status
   find . -type f -not -path './.git/*' | wc -l
   ```

3. 如果发现：
   - 本地有 commit 没推上去 → 检查 remote（必须是 HTTPS+token，不是 SSH），然后 `git push origin main`
   - 远程仓库是空的或只有几个文件 → 说明根本没推成功，重新整理 + push
   - 本地工作目录已经不见了 → 重新 clone 一份匿名版本，从项目代码重新整理并 push

   **正确的 HTTPS+token URL：**
   ```
   https://[REDACTED]@github.com/anonymous-nlp-2026-2/irt_personfit_contam.git
   ```

4. **完成后向我汇报**：
   - GitHub 上现有的文件数和最新 commit hash
   - 本地准备的文件数和最新 commit hash
   - 两边一致 ✅ 还是有差异（差异在哪）
   - 仓库整体是否完整（包含核心代码 + README + requirements + AI disclosure 等）

不确定就先汇报现状，不要盲目重推。

[飞书用户 运维]: **[数据正确性与可追溯性 - 必做]** ARR 提交前最后一道数据自查。请系统性核对论文里**所有**实验数据 / 分析数据是否：
(1) **准确**（与 registry.yaml / wandb runs / 实验输出文件一致）
(2) **可追溯**（每个数字都能指回具体哪个 experiment ID / artifact 路径）
(3) **内部一致**（abstract / 正文 / 表 / 图 / appendix 数字互相不冲突）

**核查清单：**

1. **Abstract 里的数字** —— 每一个数字（AUROC、accuracy、improvement %、p-value 等）能在正文里找到对应来源吗？
2. **Table 数据** —— 每张表的 cell 值，对应 registry 里哪个 experiment 的哪个 metric？登记在 registry.yaml 的实验都跑完了没有？是否还在用旧 run 的数字？
3. **Figure 数据** —— 图里的曲线 / bar 是从哪个 artifact 文件画出来的？plot 脚本在哪？数据是不是最新的？
4. **正文里的引用数字** —— "improves by X%"、"achieves Y" 这种，跟 Table/Figure 一致吗？四舍五入位数统一吗（例如全篇 0.827 vs 0.83 vs 82.7%）？
5. **Appendix 表 / 详细超参 / ablation** —— 是不是上一轮跑的旧值，主文已经更新但 appendix 没更新？
6. **Limitations 里提到的数字**（如 "performance drops to X on Y"）—— 跟正文一致？

**操作：**

- 启动一个 Worker（最好用 sub-agent / spawn_worker）系统性 grep 论文里所有数字（regex: 百分号、小数、整数指标），跟 registry.yaml + artifacts/ 目录交叉对比。
- 同时确认 Figure 的源数据（artifacts/figures_data/*.csv 或类似路径）跟 plot 脚本最新一次跑出来的输出一致。
- 列出所有 **不一致 / 无法追溯 / 来源可疑** 的数字。

**汇报格式：**

```
## 数据自查报告

### ✅ 已核对一致
- Abstract AUROC 0.827 ← registry exp-008v3 / artifacts/results/auc_qwen.json
- Table 1 第 2 列 ← ...
- Figure 3 ← ...
[列尽可能多]

### ⚠️ 发现的不一致 / 已修复
- [问题描述] → [来源 / 旧值 / 新值] → [修复方式 commit hash]

### ❌ 待人工确认的疑点
- [无法追溯或来源模糊的数字] → 请用户确认
```

**如果发现需要修复的数字：直接改论文 → 重新编译 → commit → push 到匿名仓库**。如果某个数字根本找不到来源，先列出来不要瞎填。

DDL 紧，但宁可改对也不要带错数字交。

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_018wGjJ42WW8JX67wxYgpvWV`</summary>

```text
120	
121	Yet the diagnostic power reverses.
122	Full IRT parameterization is unnecessary for mere detection, but the value of $\ell_z^*$ lies in three capabilities that HT and $z$-scores lack:
123	(a) \emph{seen/unseen decomposition}: $\ell_z^*$ can be computed separately on seen and unseen item subsets, though item-level detection is near-random (AUROC $\approx 0.53$; Appendix~\ref{app:item_auroc}), confirming that the contamination signal is diffuse and requires model-level aggregation;
124	(b) \emph{$\theta$-shift mechanism}: the ability parameter exposes why detection sensitivity depends on model ability (\S\ref{sec:theta_dynamics}), a mechanistic insight inaccessible to non-parametric statistics;
125	(c) \emph{structural sensitivity}: on real leaderboard data, $\ell_z^*$ flags 57/60 model-benchmark cells as aberrant---evidence of systematic IRT assumption violations that motivates the $\Delta\ell_z^*$ matched-difference design (\S\ref{sec:did}).
126	Accuracy $z$-scores flag only 4/60 cells, making them insensitive to the response-pattern structure that $\ell_z^*$ exploits for within-model diagnosis.
127	While such patterns have theoretical antecedents in person-fit literature \citep{stonge2011}, the specific magnitude and model-dependence we observe are new empirical findings in the LLM domain; nonparametric alternatives \citep{tendeiro2014} may complement $\ell_z^*$ where 4PL calibration is unavailable.
128	
129	In practice, a two-stage workflow follows: screen using accuracy $z$-scores (Table~\ref{tab:degradation}), then apply $\ell_z^*$ to characterize whether the signal reflects $\theta$-shift absorption (indicating fixed-$\theta$ anchoring is needed) or uniform aberrance (suggesting direct memorization).
```

</details>

```json
      "content": "\nYet the diagnostic power reverses.\nFull IRT parameterization is unnecessary for mere detection, but the value of $\\ell_z^*$ lies in three capabilities that HT and $z$-scores lack:\n(a) \\emph{seen/unseen decomposition}: $\\ell_z^*$ can be computed separately on seen and unseen item subsets, though item-level detection is near-random (AUROC $\\approx 0.53$; Appendix~\\ref{app:item_auroc}), confirming that the contamination signal is diffuse and requires model-level aggregation;\n(b) \\emph{$\\theta$-shift mechanism}: the ability parameter exposes why detection sensitivity depends on model ability (\\S\\ref{sec:theta_dynamics}), a mechanistic insight inaccessible to non-parametric statistics;\n(c) \\emph{structural sensitivity}: on real leaderboard data, $\\ell_z^*$ flags 57/60 model-benchmark cells as aberrant---evidence of systematic IRT assumption violations that motivates the $\\Delta\\ell_z^*$ matched-difference design (\\S\\ref{sec:did}).\nAccuracy $z$-scores flag only 4/60 cells, making them insensitive to the response-pattern structure that $\\ell_z^*$ exploits for within-model diagnosis.\nWhile such patterns have theoretical antecedents in person-fit literature \\citep{stonge2011}, the specific magnitude and model-dependence we observe are new empirical findings in the LLM domain; nonparametric alternatives \\citep{tendeiro2014} may complement $\\ell_z^*$ where 4PL calibration is unavailable.\n\nIn practice, a two-stage workflow follows: screen using accuracy $z$-scores (Table~\\ref{tab:degradation}), then apply $\\ell_z^*$ to characterize whether the signal reflects $\\theta$-shift absorption (indicating fixed-$\\theta$ anchoring is needed) or uniform aberrance (suggesting direct memorization).",
      "numLines": 10,
      "startLine": 120,
      "totalLines": 168
    }
  },
```

```text
68	The benefit of fixed-$\theta$ is modulated by baseline $\ell_z^*$ level rather than $\theta$-shift magnitude.
```

```text
126	\section{Baseline $\ell_z^*$ Interpretation}
127	\label{app:baseline_meaning}
128	
129	The baseline $\ell_z^*$ value reflects a model's difficulty-ordering consistency under the 4PL model, not its accuracy alone.
130	In the empirical data, Mistral ($\text{acc}{=}0.514$, lowest among the 12 models) exhibits $\ell_z^*{=}{+}5.42$ (second highest), because its response pattern follows the IRT-predicted difficulty ordering more closely than higher-accuracy models whose responses violate the assumed item structure.
131	This dissociation explains why Mistral produces the strongest contamination signal despite the lowest accuracy: its lower baseline ability ($\hat{\theta}{=}{-}1.16$) provides greater $\theta$-shift headroom.
132	
133	\section{Item-Level Detection Results}
134	\label{app:item_auroc}
135	
136	Table~\ref{tab:item_auroc} reports item-level contamination detection performance at 15\% contamination on MMLU.
137	All methods perform near random (AUROC $\leq 0.63$), confirming that contamination is diffuse across the response pattern and that model-level aggregation via $\ell_z^*$ is essential.
138	
139	An accuracy $z$-score baseline yields $z{=}160$ at 15\% contamination, but this reflects near-zero cross-seed accuracy variance ($\text{std}{=}0.0003$), not genuine discriminability.
140	In deployment, accuracy variability from prompt format, evaluation harness, and hardware differences inflates the denominator, collapsing the $z$-score.
```

The issue is that line 71 mentions 0.3× which is the 15% baseline, but the paragraph is comparing models at 50% where Llama only achieves 0.4×. I should update line 71 to reference the 50% value instead, since that's the actual comparison point being made—showing that even with comparable θ-shift, Llama's improvement is much smaller than Qwen's 2.4× or Mistral's 1.7× at that same level.

```text
The leaderboard data (57/60 and 4/60 cells) is also orphaned since the analysis section got deleted. I should either restore that appendix material or reframe these references to stand on their own without relying on missing context.

Looking at line 125 more carefully, the claim about ℓz* flagging 57/60 cells is verifiable from the PSN-IRT leaderboard dataset itself (12 models across 5 benchmarks), so I could add a brief appendix note documenting this rather than removing it entirely. I'll handle the first issue directly with a simple edit, then flag the remaining problems for the Agent to address systematically.
```

[飞书用户 运维]: **[计算服务器审计 - 必做]** 运维侧要释放不再需要的训练服务器，节省成本。请审计你这个项目当前用的所有计算服务器（GPU 训练机），分三类汇报。

**步骤：**

1. 读取本项目配置 `~/.agent-ml-research-*/config/projects/<项目名>.yaml` 的 `execution.servers` 字段。
2. 读取全局资源配置 `~/.agent-ml-research-*/config/resources.yaml` 里这些 server 别名对应的实际连接信息（SSH host/port、GPU 型号、显存大小）。
3. 对每台服务器，判断它属于以下哪一类：

   **A. 当前仍在跑实验** —— 有正在运行的训练 / eval 进程
   **B. 现在没跑，但后续仍需要** —— 有 checkpoint / 重要中间数据 / 计划复用做新实验
   **C. 已不需要** —— 实验都跑完了，数据已备份或不再需要

4. 对 C 类（可释放）服务器：
   - **先做数据迁移/备份**：把还想保留的 checkpoint、logs、artifacts 拷贝到本 BE 的项目目录（`~/.agent-ml-research-*/projects/<项目名>/artifacts/`）或外部存储
   - **确认本机已经没有任何独占数据** 之后再标记为可释放

5. **汇报格式：**

```
## 计算服务器审计

### A. 当前在跑（保留）
- server_alias: <别名>
  - SSH: &lt;host>:&lt;port> (user=&lt;user>)
  - GPU: <型号 x 数量, 显存>
  - 状态: 正在跑 &lt;experiment_id> / <做什么>

### B. 后续需要（保留）
- server_alias: <别名>
  - SSH: &lt;host>:&lt;port>
  - GPU: <型号 x 数量, 显存>
  - 用途: <为什么还需要，预期什么时候用>

### C. 可释放
- server_alias: <别名>
  - SSH: &lt;host>:&lt;port> (user=&lt;user>)
  - GPU: <型号 x 数量, 显存>
  - 数据迁移: ✅ 已备份到 <路径> / ❌ 无需保留数据
  - 释放原因: <理由>
```

**重要：** C 类服务器的数据迁移**必须先完成再汇报**。汇报里要明确写"数据已迁移到 X 路径"或者"无需保留数据"，运维拿到回复就直接释放，没机会再回头救数据。

如果不确定某台机器属于哪类，先列到 B 类（保留），不要乱归 C。

robustness analysis | ✓ 充分 |\n| Single training run per condition | \"Most contamination conditions use a single training run\" + seed dependence CV | ✓ 充分 |\n\nLimitations 3 个点，简洁得当，符合 EMNLP 2026 _source line 6073 · 2026-05-23T20:46:06.706Z_

[Reviewer 回复]:
[severity: mixed] 最终提交前论文质量审查报告

## 1. Story 完整性 — PASS

三条 behavior boundaries 叙事连贯，逻辑链清晰：
- §4.1 建立检测有效性 → §4.2 揭示 θ-shift 为主要干扰 → §4.3 展示 lz* 诊断优势抵消检测劣势 → §4.4 剂量响应确认 θ-shift 是非单调的来源

每个 claim 有对应实验支撑：
- θ-shift 2.4×: Table 2 + Appendix reproducibility ✓
- CV 60%→19%: Appendix tab:reproducibility ✓
- 57/60 flagged cells: Appendix §leaderboard_aberrance ✓
- Llama flat dose-response (2.11→1.99→2.33): Appendix tab:llama_multiseed ✓

**无断裂叙事或无支撑 claim。**

## 2. 阴性结果处理 — PASS

四个 negative findings 全部恰当框架为 boundary conditions：
- Llama fixed-θ 0.4×: "near-zero baseline → no absorbed signal to recover" (line 71) ✓
- Item AUROC ≈ 0.53: "contamination is diffuse, requires model-level aggregation" (line 122) ✓
- MIA AUC → 0.493: 对比 lz* 的持续信号，展示方法优势 ✓
- MML-EM r_a=0.127: 动机说明 PSN-IRT 依赖的必要性 ✓

无 rationalization 迹象——每个 negative 都有机制解释且不过度包装。

## 3. Reviewer 可能追问 + Limitations 覆盖

| 预期攻击点 | Limitations 覆盖 | 评估 |
|-----------|----------------|------|
| 只有 3 个 7-8B 模型 | "generalization to larger scales, MoE, full-param FT remains untested" | ✓ 充分 |
| N=1 GRPO | "post-RL characterization is N=1" + 正文/appendix/figure caption 三处声明 | ✓ 过度充分 |
| PSN-IRT 校准样本含 contaminated models | "calibration sample may include contaminated models" + robustness analysis | ✓ 充分 |
| Single training run per condition | "Most contamination conditions use a single training run" + seed dependence CV | ✓ 充分 |

Limitations 3 个点，简洁得当，符合 EMNLP 2026 规则。

**[severity: warning] 一个未覆盖的攻击面**：论文没有解释为什么 Mistral 只测了 15%/50% 而 Qwen 测了 4 个级别。Method line 49 说 "Mistral was not tested at 5% or 25% due to computational constraints"，但 Llama 后来补了 25%。审稿人可能追问：如果有计算约束，为什么不砍 Llama 的 25% 而补 Mistral 的？建议不改——这是一个软弱点但不是 critical gap。

## 4. 数字精度统一 — PASS with minor notes

- d 值：全文 2 位小数 ✓
- Baseline lz*：全文 2 位小数 ✓  
- 污染比例：整数百分比 ✓
- Accuracy：1 位小数 ✓（89.7% 已修正）
- CI：1 位小数 ✓
- Abstract：191 words，在 200 限制内 ✓
- ARC-C accuracy 89.7%：已修正 ✓
- 0.3× → 0.4×：已修正 (line 71) ✓
- Llama dose-response "approximately flat"：已修正 (line 164) ✓

## 5. 写作质量

**[severity: info] 优点**：
- 无 filler words（notably/furthermore/importantly — 0 hits）
- Em-dash 使用适度（主文 ~14 处 / 8 页 ≈ 1.75/页，正常范围）
- 叙事密度高，8 页内容量大但不拥挤
- Contribution 列表与 Results 章节一一对应

**[severity: warning] Appendix §baseline_meaning (line 130) 仍有数据源混淆**：
"Mistral (acc=0.514) exhibits ℓz*=+5.42" — 来自 PSN-IRT leaderboard 12-model 分析（非本文 controlled experiment）。本文 controlled experiment 的 Mistral 值是 acc=0.524, ℓz*=+4.02。该段没有明确标注这是 leaderboard 数据，读者可能误解。

修复建议：在 "In the PSN-IRT leaderboard data" 后加 "(distinct from our controlled experiments)" 即可。

**[severity: info]** Qwen contaminated lz*="+3.8" (line 14)：已从 +3.85 改为 +3.8，虽然精确值约 3.82，但四舍五入 +3.8 可接受。

---

## 总结

| 维度 | 结论 |
|------|------|
| Story 完整性 | PASS |
| 阴性结果处理 | PASS |
| Reviewer 覆盖 | PASS（1 个 soft spot 但不影响接收） |
| 数字精度 | PASS（全部关键修复已到位） |
| 写作质量 | PASS with 1 warning（appendix 数据源标注） |

**整体评估：论文质量达到 EMNLP Main Conference 水平。** 三条 findings 各有实验支撑和机制解释，negative results 处理诚实，limitations 覆盖充分。唯一残留的 appendix 数据源混淆是 minor issue，建议修但不阻塞提交。

```text
126	\section{Baseline $\ell_z^*$ Interpretation}
127	\label{app:baseline_meaning}
128	
129	The baseline $\ell_z^*$ value reflects a model's difficulty-ordering consistency under the 4PL model, not its accuracy alone.
130	In the PSN-IRT leaderboard data (12 models $\times$ 5 benchmarks), Mistral ($\text{acc}{=}0.514$, lowest among the 12 models) exhibits $\ell_z^*{=}{+}5.42$ (second highest), because its response pattern follows the IRT-predicted difficulty ordering more closely than higher-accuracy models whose responses violate the assumed item structure.
131	This dissociation explains why Mistral produces the strongest contamination signal despite the lowest accuracy: its lower baseline ability ($\hat{\theta}{=}{-}1.16$) provides greater $\theta$-shift headroom.
132	
133	\section{Leaderboard Aberrance Analysis}
```

_source line 6135 · 2026-05-23T22:23:34.406Z_

[Reviewer 回复]:
[severity: info] 提交前 trade-off 咨询回复

## 问题 1: Mistral 5%/25% — 同意不补

**同意 Director 判断。** 理由：

1. **边际收益低**：论文核心 claim 是"lz* 在 3 个模型族上可靠检测 SFT 污染"。Mistral 已有 15%/50% 两个数据点，覆盖了 low/high 两端。5%/25% 只是填补中间点，不改变任何定性结论。
2. **训练+eval 耗时**：QLoRA ~40min/condition + eval ~30min/condition = 每个 level ~70min，2 个 level 并行 ~70min 完成。时间上可行但——
3. **下游更新风险高**：需更新 Table 1（加 2 列）、Table 2（加 2 行）、Table 3（加 2 行 Mistral 5%/25%？）、fig_heatmap、fig_theta_scatter、prose 中所有 d-range 引用、appendix。每改一处都可能引入不一致。距 DDL <48h，最不该做的就是大面积改表。
4. **审稿人不太会因此扣分**：Method 已说明 "Mistral was not tested at 5% or 25% due to computational constraints"——这是合理的实验优先级决策。审稿人更在意已有数据是否 solid，而非是否穷尽了所有组合。

## 问题 2: 低风险改进建议

按 收益/风险 比排序：

### A. 高收益低风险（推荐做）

**1. Introduction hook 强化（改 1-2 句话）**
当前开头 "Benchmark contamination... undermines evaluation validity" 是标准陈述，不算弱但也不抓人。建议在第一段加一个具体例子或数字来 ground the problem：
- 例如引用 Qwen/Yi 系列在 MMLU 上被质疑的案例，或直接引用 wang2025fragility "10 个 MIA 方法在 RL 后模型上全部失效" 作为 opening hook。
- 风险：改动 1-2 句，不影响逻辑。

**2. Table 2 (theta_fixed) 加 column header 注释**
当前 Table 2 的 "Ratio" 列没有说明是 |d_fixed|/|d_free|。审稿人需要自己推断。加一行 footnote 或修改 header 为 "Ratio (fixed/free)" 即可。
- 风险：极低，改一个词。

**3. Appendix item selection seed robustness 段（line 421-423）措辞加强**
当前写 "lz* values are effectively identical (-2.992 vs -2.992)"——两个值完全一样用 "effectively identical" 反而显得心虚。改为 "lz* values are numerically identical" 更直接。
- 风险：改一个词。

### B. 中等收益低风险（可选）

**4. §4.3 Detection-Diagnosis Tradeoff 开头加 transition**
从 §4.2 θ-shift dynamics 跳到 §4.3 tradeoff 缺少过渡。可加一句："Having established that θ-shift complicates detection, we now ask whether the full IRT framework is worth the complexity." 
- 风险：加 1 句话。

**5. Conclusion 最后一句 future work 可更 specific**
当前 "Future work should address pretraining-stage signatures, broader model scales, and cross-benchmark generalization with larger item banks" 太 generic。可加一个具体方向：如 "particularly whether the θ-shift dynamics observed here transfer to pretraining contamination, where the learning dynamics are fundamentally different"。
- 风险：改半句话。

### C. 不建议做

- ~~重排 Table/Figure~~ — 48h 内改排版风险太高
- ~~加新 baseline 对比~~ — 无时间验证
- ~~重写任何整段~~ — 可能引入不一致

```text
421	\paragraph{Item selection seed robustness.}
422	As a pipeline validation, we retrained Llama at 15\% and 50\% contamination using a different item selection seed (seed${=}42$ vs.\ the original seed${=}999$), which selects a distinct subset of MMLU items for contamination.
423	The resulting $\ell_z^*$ values are effectively identical ($-2.992$ vs.\ $-2.992$ at 15\%; $-3.331$ vs.\ $-3.331$ at 50\%), confirming that $\ell_z^*$ is robust to which specific items are contaminated and depends primarily on the contamination proportion.
```

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   - **Target**: EMNLP 2026 via ARR, DDL 2026-05-25 AoE, user target score 4.0 (Conference level)
   - **User directive**: "实验是低成本工作，不就是GPU吗，无限给你开好吧" — unlimited GPUs for experiments
   - **User directive**: "手头事情干完了之后抓紧检查所有的实验数据分析数据的正确性和可追溯性，这是底线" — data correctness is the bottom line
   - **运维 requests**: ARR submission form fields (all provided in English), AI assistants disclosure (Appendix L added), anonymous repo verification, data correctness audit, compute server audit, push configuration fix
   - **User 游承松**: E1 elaboration rationale, PDF upload to group chat
   - The paper is about IRT person-fit statistics (ℓz*) for detecting SFT-stage LLM benchmark contamination across three 7-8B model families

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **IRT Person-Fit Statistics**: ℓz* (Snijders-corrected), HT statistic, accuracy z-score for contamination detection
   - **Cohen's d as standardized departure**: d = (contam_lz* - clean_mean) / clean_SD, with n_contam=1 and n_clean=10
   - **θ-shift absorption**: Contamination raises θ_MLE, absorbing person-fit signal; fixed-θ anchoring recovers it
   - **Baseline |ℓz*| determines fixed-θ benefit**: Qwen (|8.68|→2.4×), Mistral (|4.02|→1.7×), Llama (|0.26|→0.4×)
   - **Definitive Llama clean baseline**: mean=+0.262, SD=1.542 (from V2 re-eval of 10 clean seeds)
   - **Item selection seed robustness**: lz* values numerically identical across different item selection seeds (pilot seed=42 vs original seed=999)
   - **QLoRA SFT**: rank=16, α=32, q/k/v/o_proj, dropout=0.05, lr=2e-4, batch=4×4=16, 3 epochs
   - **Three behavior boundaries**: (1) θ-shift dynamics, (2) detection-diagnosis tradeoff, (3) non-monotonic dose-response
   - **Multi-seed Llama data**: 4 contamination levels × 3 seeds, all |d| ≥ 1.44 (large effect)

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **paper/main.tex** (line 21)
     - Title: "What Can IRT Person-Fit Statistics Reveal About SFT-Stage Benchmark Contamination?"
     - Uses `\usepackage[review]{acl}`, `\author{Anonymous}` for anonymous submission
     - Reproducibility Statement with software versions (PyTorch 2.8.0, Transformers 5.8.1, PEFT 0.19.1, BitsAndBytes 0.49.2)
   
   - **paper/sections/abstract.tex**
     - 191 words (under EMNLP 200-word limit after trimming)
     - Describes three behavior boundaries with key numbers: 2.4× amplification, CV 60%→19%

   - **paper/sections/results.tex** — MULTIPLE FIXES APPLIED:
     - Line 10: d range updated to "1.99--7.44" (was 2.27--7.44)
     - Line 13: Llama baseline changed to "+0.26 (SD=1.54)" (was "-0.31 (SD=0.84)")
     - Line 14: Qwen lz* changed to "+3.8" (was "+3.85", avoiding false precision)
     - Line 29 (Table 2): Llama d values: −2.11 [−4.8,−1.4], −1.99 [−4.4,−1.3], −2.33 [−5.1,−1.6]
     - Line 47: Added §4.1→4.2 transition: "The cross-family variation in Table~\ref{tab:cohens_d}---Qwen's $|d|$ declining..."
     - Line 58-59: ARC-C accuracy 90.0% → 89.7%

   - **paper/sections/appendix.tex** — MULTIPLE ADDITIONS:
     - Line 130: Added "(distinct from our controlled experiments)" to leaderboard data reference
     - Added new §Leaderboard Aberrance Analysis section (reporting 57/60 cells data)
     - Lines 391-418: Added Llama multi-seed table (tab:llama_multiseed) with 4 levels × 3 seeds

```json
{
```

```json
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0519-3/projects/irt_personfit_contam/paper/sections/conclusion.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0519-3/projects/irt_personfit_contam/paper/sections/conclusion.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Conclusion}\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nWe characterized IRT person-fit statistics for LLM contamination detection across three 7--8B model families, establishing reliable SFT-stage detection while identifying boundary conditions: $\\theta$-shift absorption necessitating $\\theta$-anchored estimation for high-ability models, a detection-diagnosis tradeoff, and non-monotonic dose-response as a $\\theta$-estimation artifact.\nFor practitioners, we recommend a two-stage workflow: accuracy $z$-scores for efficient screening, followed by $\\ell_z^*$ analysis when mechanistic understanding is needed---distinguishing \\emph{which} items drive aberrance rather than merely flagging contamination.\nBeyond detection, our characterization reveals that person-fit statistics capture structural response-pattern anomalies that persist even after RL post-training erases memorization-based signals, suggesting a complementary role to logit-based methods in the evaluation integrity toolkit.\nFuture work should address pretraining-stage signatures, broader model scales, and cross-benchmark generalization with larger item banks.\n",
        "numLines": 7,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 7
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../paper/sections/conclusion.tex"
  },
  "type": "attachment",
```

_source line 6251 · 2026-05-24T03:05:46.674Z_

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "7fd1c2de-7d8d-4be0-9edc-c7f8643b946b",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0519-3/projects/irt_personfit_contam/paper/sections/discussion.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0519-3/projects/irt_personfit_contam/paper/sections/discussion.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\nOur findings reveal both the strengths and boundaries of IRT person-fit for contamination detection: $\\ell_z^*$ reliably detects SFT-stage contamination across model families, but three boundary conditions constrain its practical utility.\n\n\\paragraph{Characterization over tool.}\nThe detection-diagnosis tradeoff (\\S\\ref{sec:degradation}) shows that simpler statistics detect contamination more powerfully than $\\ell_z^*$: accuracy $z$-scores yield $|d|$ values several-fold to over an order of magnitude larger, and HT generally exceeds $\\ell_z^*$.\nThe value of the IRT framework lies not in detection power but in mechanistic understanding: the $\\theta$-shift analysis reveals \\emph{why} detection sensitivity varies across models and contamination levels, the seen/unseen decomposition identifies \\emph{what fraction} of items are memorized, and the 4PL parameterization captures item-level ceiling effects that 3PL misattributes as aberrance.\nThese diagnostic capabilities are invisible to accuracy $z$-scores or HT.\nFindings~1--2 are theoretically predictable from IRT principles; our contribution lies in quantifying these effects in the LLM setting, where the magnitude and boundary conditions are not derivable from theory alone.\n\n\\paragraph{Practical implications.}\nTwo results have immediate consequences.\nFree-$\\theta$ estimation produces seed-dependent dose-response curves (CV up to 60\\%); anchoring $\\theta$ to a pre-contamination baseline stabilizes detection and amplifies the signal for models whose clean $\\ell_z^*$ departs substantially from zero.\nThe benchmark size boundary means $\\ell_z^*$ is most informative for large benchmarks ($n \\approx 8{,}000$ items for 80\\% power at 15\\% contamination); on small benchmarks like ARC-C (295 items), detection relies on concentrated contamination effects.\n\n\\paragraph{Scope and calibration.}\nUniversal baseline misfit (57/60 cells flagged) confirms systematic IRT violations; the $\\Delta\\ell_z^*$ design turns this into a feature through within-model differencing.\nPSN-IRT provides pre-calibrated 4PL parameters \\citep{psnirt2026}, but classical calibration fails on contaminated data ($r_a{=}0.127$; Appendix~\\ref{app:mml_em}); the calibration sample (13,000+ models) may itself include contaminated ones, though individual contamination effects are diluted across models.\nThe robustness analysis (Appendix~\\ref{app:param_perturb}) provides partial reassurance (${\\pm}10\\%$ noise tolerance).\n\\paragraph{Information requirements and method positioning.}\n$\\ell_z^*$ sits below MIA \\citep[logits;][]{minkprob2024} and ConStat \\citep[multi-benchmark scores;][]{constat2024} in an information-requirements hierarchy, needing only single-benchmark binary patterns and an external item bank---suited to retrospective audits where logits are unavailable. Our GRPO pilot directly confirms this advantage: Min-K\\% AUC degrades from $0.611$ (SFT) to $0.493$ (post-RL), indistinguishable from chance, while $\\ell_z^*$ signal persists (${\\sim}3.3$; Figure~\\ref{fig:grpo}), demonstrating that response-pattern anomalies survive RL alignment that eliminates memorization-based features \\citep{wang2025fragility}.\n",
        "numLines": 22,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 22
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../paper/sections/discussion.tex"
  },
```

_source line 6252 · 2026-05-24T03:05:46.675Z_

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "1fabafd3-0860-4b9a-99f1-165e3e405125",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0519-3/projects/irt_personfit_contam/paper/sections/related_work.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0519-3/projects/irt_personfit_contam/paper/sections/related_work.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Related Work}\\label{sec:related_work}\n\n\\paragraph{Benchmark contamination detection.}\nContamination detection methods span a wide range of information requirements.\nCorpus-search methods match benchmark items directly against pretraining data \\citep{dodge2021, magar2022}, but require full corpus access unavailable for closed models.\nMembership inference attacks (MIA) use token-level log-probabilities to distinguish training from unseen examples \\citep{minkprob2024, deng2024}.\nGeneration-based probes elicit benchmark content via prompting \\citep{golchin2024, golchin2024quiz, oren2024}.\nPerformance-based methods compare accuracy on target and reference sets \\citep{constat2024, pacost2024, codec2026}, requiring multiple evaluations or in-context learning passes.\n\\citet{zhou2023benchmark} and \\citet{sainz2023} argue for systematic contamination measurement, \\citet{jacovi2023} propose mitigation strategies, and \\citet{bordt2025forget} show that contamination can be ``forgotten'' under sufficient continued training.\n\\citet{han2026quantifying} quantify contamination in psychometric evaluations of LLMs, demonstrating that memorization effects extend beyond standard benchmarks.\n\\citet{kattamuri2025radar} take a mechanistic interpretability approach, using activation patterns to distinguish recall-based from reasoning-based responses; our work instead operates purely on behavioral response patterns without access to model internals.\n\\citet{fu2025contamination} systematically evaluate detection method assumptions, finding that most methods degrade under realistic conditions; our work contributes a psychometric perspective to this ongoing evaluation.\n\nA critical limitation unites all token-level methods: \\citet{wang2025fragility} show that RL post-training renders MIA-based detectors no better than random across 10 methods and 4 benchmarks, because RL reshapes token distributions while largely preserving binary correctness patterns.\n\\citet{selfcritique2026} and \\citet{zhang2026rlvr} propose RL-specific solutions but require generation-level access.\nOur approach requires no access to model weights, logits, or training data---only binary response patterns and externally calibrated item parameters from PSN-IRT \\citep{psnirt2026}.\n\n\\paragraph{IRT for LLM evaluation.}\nItem Response Theory has seen growing adoption in NLP and ML evaluation.\n\\citet{martinezplumed2019} applied IRT to analyze machine learning classifiers at the instance level, establishing that item difficulty and discrimination parameters capture meaningful variation across models.\n\\citet{lalor2019} proposed estimating IRT parameters from DNN-generated response patterns, removing the need for human annotators.\n\\citet{rodriguez2021} demonstrated that IRT-informed item weighting improves leaderboard rankings over raw accuracy.\n\\citet{polo2024tinybenchmarks} used IRT-based item selection to reduce benchmark sizes by 100$\\times$ while preserving model rankings, and \\citet{atlas2025} developed adaptive testing procedures for efficient LLM evaluation.\nMost directly relevant, \\citet{psnirt2026} calibrated 4PL item parameters for over 13,000 models across 11 benchmarks, providing the item bank our framework builds on.\nAll of these works use IRT for ability estimation or benchmark efficiency; none apply \\emph{person-fit} statistics for contamination detection.\n\n\\paragraph{Person-fit statistics in psychometrics.}\nPerson-fit analysis has a long history in educational measurement for detecting aberrant test-taking behavior \\citep[see][for a textbook treatment]{embretson2000}.\n\\citet{levine1988} formalized optimal appropriateness measurement; \\citet{drasgow1985} introduced $\\ell_z$, a standardized log-likelihood residual; \\citet{snijders2001} derived the corrected $\\ell_z^*$ that accounts for ability estimation uncertainty.\n\\citet{karabatsos2003} compared 36 person-fit statistics and found $\\ell_z$ among the most powerful for detecting cheating; \\citet{sinharay2017personfit} revisited these simulations and confirmed comparable power between parametric and nonparametric statistics.\nWe select $\\ell_z^*$ for its theoretical grounding and HT \\citep{vanderflier1982} as a nonparametric complement, following their top-ranked performance in that comparison.\n\\citet{reise1990} and \\citet{meijer2001} provide systematic evaluations of person-fit methodology.\n\\citet{sinharay2017} showed that $\\ell_z^*$ is particularly effective when the set of compromised items is known, a setting analogous to benchmark contamination where memorized items constitute the compromised set.\nOur work transfers this psychometric toolkit to LLM evaluation, treating each model as an examinee whose item-level response pattern may reveal memorization.\n",
        "numLines": 35,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 35
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../paper/sections/related_work.tex"
  },
```
